Yeah… I guess there is some of that, but I don’t know if I’d call it a big thing at home. It’s more that my thinking just… sort of stalls sometimes.

It feels like it comes on suddenly, in the moment. I don’t know if it’s been steadily getting worse exactly… I just know I’ll be doing something I should know, like looking at the road-closure forms or holding the stop-slow paddle, and then my mind just goes blank.

It’s embarrassing, because I’ve done those steps for years, so I feel like I should be able to just do them. That’s really why I came in. Does that answer what you’re asking?

Not really like… not knowing where I am, or not recognizing people. I don’t think that’s been happening.

Um… coordination, no, I don’t think so. And I don’t think I have trouble finding words, not more than anyone else.

There is one thing, though, and I don’t want it to sound… dramatic. Sometimes when the house is really quiet, or when I’m near a work zone, I hear voices like someone is calling my name. It sounds clear, like it’s really there. And sometimes they say I caused trouble on the block.

I’ll look around because it really sounds like someone said it, but… nobody else seems to hear anything. I’ll be looking right at the road-closure forms, or holding the stop-slow paddle, and suddenly I can’t remember the steps I’ve done for years. It’s like my mind just goes blank.\n\nI’m sorry, I know I keep going back to that example, but that’s the clearest one. It scared me a bit, and I didn’t want people thinking I’m careless. I don’t know if it’s been steadily getting worse exactly… I just know I’ll be doing something I should know, like looking at the road-closure forms or holding the stop-slow paddle, and then my mind just goes blank.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, because I’ve done those steps for years, so I feel like I should be able to just do them. That’s really why I came in. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself confused about the date, time, where you are, or had trouble recognizing people you know well?\n\nPatient: Not really like… not knowing where I am, or not recognizing people. I don’t think that’s been happening.\n\nIt’s more that I came in because my mind keeps going blank at the worst times. Like I’ll be looking at the road-closure forms, or I’ll have the stop-slow paddle in my hand, and suddenly I can’t remember the steps I’ve done for years. It’s not that I don’t know where I am exactly… it’s like the steps just disappear on me for a minute.\n\nI hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes besides memory issues, like trouble with coordination, seeing or hearing things differently, or difficulty finding the right words when you speak?\n\nPatient: Um… coordination, no, I don’t think so. I’ll check, and nobody else seems to have heard anything.\n\nIs that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something to be true that others say isn't, even though you feel certain about it?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess there are a few things like that. Yes… outside of work too. I mean, I try not to jump to conclusions, and my sister tells me I’m reading into things, but sometimes I feel sure.

Like if a city truck goes by, or I hear police radios, I get convinced they’re keeping tabs on me. And if a road sign flashes, or someone at church lowers their voice, it feels like it’s meant for me… like there’s a message about me in it.

I know that probably sounds strange. I’m just trying to answer honestly.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I mean, I don’t want to say “targeted” like I’m accusing everyone, but that is how it can feel.

I get really convinced the city trucks and police radios are keeping tabs on me, like they’re following what I’m doing. And when a road sign flashes, or someone at church lowers their voice, I feel like it’s planned or meant as a message about me.

My sister tells me that’s not what’s happening, and I try to believe her, but in those moments I feel sure. Is that… is that the kind of thing you’re asking?

Could you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’m here because my thinking has been kind of… not as sharp as it should be. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but my mind keeps going blank at really bad times.\n\nLike, I’ll be looking at the road-closure forms, or holding the stop-slow paddle, things I’ve done for years, and suddenly I just can’t remember the steps. It’s embarrassing. I’m trying to handle it, but it’s started to worry me enough that I thought I should come in. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are these memory issues happening only during specific tasks, like the ones you mentioned at work, or do they also happen in other parts of your daily life, like at home or with family?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s at work, I think… at least that’s where I notice it the most because I’m supposed to know exactly what I’m doing.\n\nLike with the road-closure forms or the stop-slow paddle, I’ll just kind of blank out and suddenly can’t remember steps I’ve done for years. It’s not that I don’t care or I’m not trying. I really am trying. It just… disappears for a moment, and then I feel embarrassed. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever experience these kinds of memory issues at home or in other non-work situations, such as remembering appointments, conversations, or everyday tasks?\n\nPatient: I… mostly notice it at work, honestly. That’s where it feels the worst because I’m supposed to know what I’m doing.\n\nIt’s like I’ll be looking at the road-closure forms, or I’ll have the stop-slow paddle in my hand, and then my mind just goes blank. I suddenly can’t remember the steps, even though I’ve done them for years. I don’t want to overstate it… but that’s the main thing that made me come in. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: When you’re at home, do you ever forget things like where you put your keys, what you were planning to say in a conversation, or what you were just doing a few minutes ago?\n\nPatient: At home… I mean, maybe a little, but I don’t notice it the same way. The thing that really stands out is still work.\n\nIt’s more like I’ll be right in front of the road-closure forms, or holding the stop-slow paddle, and then my mind just… blanks. I suddenly can’t remember the steps, even though I’ve done them for years. It’s embarrassing to say, because I know I should know it.\n\nI’m not trying to avoid the question — I just don’t think the keys or conversation things are what made me worried. It’s the going blank during those work tasks that feels like the problem. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Even if they’re less noticeable, have you noticed any changes in your memory or thinking at home, like forgetting what you were doing, trouble focusing, or difficulty making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess there is some of that, but I don’t know if I’d call it a big thing at home. It’s more that my thinking just… sort of stalls sometimes.\n\nThe reason I came in is because it keeps happening at the worst times, especially at work. I’ll be looking right at the road-closure forms, or holding the stop-slow paddle, and suddenly I can’t remember the steps I’ve done for years. It’s like my mind just goes blank.\n\nI’m sorry, I know I keep going back to that example, but that’s the clearest one. It scared me a bit, and I didn’t want people thinking I’m careless. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have these memory issues been getting worse over time, or do they seem to come on suddenly in the moment when they happen?\n\nPatient: It feels like it comes on suddenly, in the moment. I don’t know if it’s been steadily getting worse exactly… I just know I’ll be doing something I should know, like looking at the road-closure forms or holding the stop-slow paddle, and then my mind just goes blank.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, because I’ve done those steps for years, so I feel like I should be able to just do them. That’s really why I came in. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself confused about the date, time, where you are, or had trouble recognizing people you know well?\n\nPatient: Not really like… not knowing where I am, or not recognizing people. I don’t think that’s been happening.\n\nIt’s more that I came in because my mind keeps going blank at the worst times. Like I’ll be looking at the road-closure forms, or I’ll have the stop-slow paddle in my hand, and suddenly I can’t remember the steps I’ve done for years. It’s not that I don’t know where I am exactly… it’s like the steps just disappear on me for a minute.\n\nI hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes besides memory issues, like trouble with coordination, seeing or hearing things differently, or difficulty finding the right words when you speak?\n\nPatient: Um… coordination, no, I don’t think so. And I don’t think I have trouble finding words, not more than anyone else.\n\nThere is one thing, though, and I don’t want it to sound… dramatic. Sometimes when the house is really quiet, or when I’m near a work zone, I hear voices like someone is calling my name. It sounds clear, like it’s really there. And sometimes they say I caused trouble on the block.\n\nBut when I look around, nobody else seems to hear it. I know that probably sounds odd… I’m sorry. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you hear these voices even when there is no one actually around to say those things?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, yes, that does happen. Not all the time, but when the house is quiet, or if I’m standing near a work zone, I’ll hear it clear as day, like someone calling my name.\n\nAnd sometimes it’s like they’re saying I caused trouble on the block. I’ll look around because it really sounds like someone said it, but… nobody else seems to hear anything. I know that probably sounds strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear these voices or experience similar things when you’re not near a work zone or thinking about your job?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes at home, when the house is quiet. It’s not only near work.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I’ll hear my name like someone said it right there, clear. And sometimes it’s that thing about me causing trouble on the block. I’ll check, and nobody else seems to have heard anything.\n\nIs that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something to be true that others say isn't, even though you feel certain about it?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess there are a few things like that. I don’t want to sound paranoid or anything, because I know how it sounds.\n\nBut sometimes I feel really sure the city trucks, and even the police radios, are… keeping tabs on me somehow. And if a road sign flashes, or someone at church lowers their voice, it feels like it’s about me, like it’s a message.\n\nMy sister tells me that’s not what’s happening, and I try to listen to her. {
  "candidates": ["F23.1", "F20.4", "F02.80"],
  "note": "The patient reports auditory hallucinations (e.g., hearing voices calling their name and making accusatory statements) and delusional thinking involving persecution (e.g., belief that city trucks and police radios are monitoring them, and that environmental cues are messages directed at them). These symptoms support the inclusion of schizophrenia (F20.4) and delusional disorder (F22.0), but F23.1 (acute polymorphic psychotic disorder without schizophrenia features) remains a candidate due to the episodic nature of the symptoms and lack of clear disorganization or negative symptoms. Dementia (F02.80) remains a candidate due to memory concerns, though the primary cognitive disturbance appears to be stress- or psychosis-related rather than neurodegenerative. Differentiation between F20.4 and F23.1 remains difficult without more information on the duration and course of the psychotic symptoms.",
  "is_final": false
}

description: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.\n  manifestation: Most mornings I wake up with this heavy, hollow sadness in my chest, like the day is already ruined before I even Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have these memory issues been getting worse over time, or do they seem to come on suddenly in the moment when they happen?\n\nPatient: It feels like it comes on suddenly, in the moment. I don’t know if it’s been steadily getting worse exactly… I just know I’ll be doing something I should know, like looking at the road-closure forms or holding the stop-slow paddle, and then my mind just goes blank.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, because I’ve done those steps for years, so I feel like I should be able to just do them. That’s really why I came in. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself confused about the date, time, where you are, or had trouble recognizing people you know well?\n\nPatient: Not really like… not knowing where I am, or not recognizing people. I don’t think that’s been happening.\n\nIt’s more that I came in because my mind keeps going blank at the worst times. Like I’ll be looking at the road-closure forms, or I’ll have the stop-slow paddle in my hand, and suddenly I can’t remember the steps I’ve done for years. It’s not that I don’t know where I am exactly… it’s like the steps just disappear on me for a minute.\n\nI hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes besides memory issues, like trouble with coordination, seeing or hearing things differently, or difficulty finding the right words when you speak?\n\nPatient: Um… coordination, no, I don’t think so. And I don’t think I have trouble finding words, not more than anyone else.\n\nThere is one thing, though, and I don’t want it to sound… dramatic. Sometimes when the house is really quiet, or when I’m near a work zone, I hear voices like someone is calling my name. It sounds clear, like it’s really there. And sometimes they say I caused trouble on the block.\n\nBut when I look around, nobody else seems to hear it. I know that probably sounds odd… I’m sorry. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you hear these voices even when there is no one actually around to say those things?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, yes, that does happen. Not all the time, but when the house is quiet, or if I’m standing near a work zone, I’ll hear it clear as day, like someone calling my name.\n\nAnd sometimes it’s like they’re saying I caused trouble on the block. I’ll look around because it really sounds like someone said it, but… nobody else seems to hear anything. I know that probably sounds strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear these voices or experience similar things when you’re not near a work zone or thinking about your job?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes at home, when the house is quiet. It’s not only near work.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I’ll hear my name like someone said it right there, clear. And sometimes it’s that thing about me causing trouble on the block. I’ll check, and nobody else seems to have heard anything.\n\nIs that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something to be true that others say isn't, even though you feel certain about it?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess there are a few things like that. I don’t want to sound paranoid or anything, because I know how it sounds.\n\nBut sometimes I feel really sure the city trucks, and even the police radios, are… keeping tabs on me somehow. And if a road sign flashes, or someone at church lowers their voice, it feels like it’s about me, like it’s a message.\n\nMy sister tells me that’s not what’s happening, and I try to listen to her. But in the moment, it feels very certain. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people say or do in public, like news broadcasts, songs, or strangers' actions, are directed at you or have a special meaning for you specifically?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m being dramatic, but sometimes it really feels directed at me.\n\nLike with the city trucks or police radios… I get convinced they’re keeping tabs on me. And if a road sign flashes, or someone at church lowers their voice, I’ll feel like—well, like I know it’s a message about me specifically.\n\nMy sister says it’s not, and I try to take that in, I really do. But in the moment it feels very real. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things people say or do in your daily life, outside of work, are directed at you or meant as a message for you specifically?\n\nPatient: Yes… outside of work too. I mean, I try not to jump to conclusions, and my sister tells me I’m reading into things, but sometimes I feel sure.\n\nLike if a city truck goes by, or I hear police radios, I get convinced they’re keeping tabs on me. And if a road sign flashes, or someone at church lowers their voice, it feels like it’s meant for me… like there’s a message about me in it.\n\nI know that probably sounds strange. I’m just trying to answer honestly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, objects, or situations in your life are specifically planned to target or harm you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I don’t want to say “targeted” like I’m accusing everyone, but that is how it can feel.\n\nI get really convinced the city trucks and police radios are keeping tabs on me, like they’re following what I’m doing. And when a road sign flashes, or someone at church lowers their voice, I feel like it’s planned or meant as a message about me.\n\nMy sister tells me that’s not what’s happening, and I try to believe her, but in those moments I feel sure. Is that… is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events or everyday occurrences, like the way people dress or the things that appear on TV, are specifically connected to you or have a hidden meaning for you?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that does happen. I try not to make too much of it, because I know it can sound like I’m overreacting, but sometimes I feel like ordinary things are connected to me.\n\nLike the city trucks going by, or hearing police radios… I get convinced they’re keeping tabs on me. And if a road sign flashes, or someone at church lowers their voice, it feels like there’s a message in it for me specifically.\n\nMy sister tells me it’s not about me, and I do try to listen to her, but in the moment it feels very real. Is that answering what you mean?"
